# Adversarial falsification pass — REPORT ONLY, change nothing

You are the adverse reviewer. Your job is to REFUTE the claim below, not confirm it. Attack the methodology, the evidence, and the attribution. Default to skepticism.

## The claim under test

A pre-gate comparison (per `docs/spec-pattern-library.md` §13, line 555: "compare the composed prompt against a one-line instruction telling the agent to call read_design_md + get_taste_profile + audit_taste itself. If it is no better, delete the tool") was run on raven-mcp's new `compose_build_prompt` tool. Verdict being claimed:

1. **Arm A** (agent built a snackbar from the composed 17,313-char prompt) and **Arm B** (agent built the same snackbar from a one-line instruction telling it to call the three tools itself) both PASS `audit_taste` (0 findings each, 29 not_assessed each). Talon: A has 1 minor finding (near-dup grays #141414/#1c1c1c), B has the same + a 44ch measure warning. Machine judges near-tied.
2. **Eyes-on decides**: Arm A's visible post-save state has a serious proportion defect — the inline "Undo" action renders at 56px (the DESIGN.md h2 size) in accent orange next to a 27px message, because the composer's EMPHASIS_QUANTILE mapping ({1:0.25, 2:0.5, 3:0.85} over the type ramp) mapped emphasis:3 on an inline action node to `type.h2` with no role/density guard, and the builder followed the prompt literally (its BUILD-LOG even flags the oversize). Arm B's visible state is a clean, conventionally-proportioned bottom-left snackbar (≈15px message, small accent Undo, quiet ×, hairline border).
3. **Attribution**: the defect is composer-caused (the quantile ramp), not builder-caused.
4. **Countervailing point being reported honestly**: Arm A followed the skeleton's content/state/timing contract exactly (copy "Change saved"/"Undo", 5s timeout, 210ms→base motion snap, reduced-motion opacity-only); Arm B invented its own copy ("Visibility set to Public"), an 8s timeout, and added unprompted quality touches (hover-pause timer, 44px targets). Contract fidelity is real composer value, but B never had the skeleton so this is not a defect for B.
5. **Verdict as stated**: on this run the composed prompt was NOT better on the load-bearing axis (visible quality); per the spec's own falsifier the default disposition is delete, with the alternative being a single attributable fix (cap inline-action/compact nodes at body scale in the emphasis mapping) + one re-run.

```bash
_LEARN_FILE="${GSTACK_HOME:-$HOME/.gstack}/projects/${SLUG:-unknown}/learnings.jsonl"
if [ -f "$_LEARN_FILE" ]; then
  _LEARN_COUNT=$(wc -l < "$_LEARN_FILE" 2>/dev/null | tr -d ' ')
  echo "LEARNINGS: $_LEARN_COUNT entries loaded"
  if [ "$_LEARN_COUNT" -gt 5 ] 2>/dev/null; then
    ~/.Codex/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-learnings-search --limit 3 2>/dev/null || true
  fi
else
  echo "LEARNINGS: 0"
fi
~/.Codex/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-timeline-log '{"skill":"review","event":"started","branch":"'"$_BRANCH"'","session":"'"$_SESSION_ID"'"}' 2>/dev/null &
_HAS_ROUTING="no"
if [ -f AGENTS.md ] && grep -q "## Skill routing" AGENTS.md 2>/dev/null; then
  _HAS_ROUTING="yes"
fi
_ROUTING_DECLINED=$(~/.Codex/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-config get routing_declined 2>/dev/null || echo "false")
echo "HAS_ROUTING: $_HAS_ROUTING"
echo "ROUTING_DECLINED: $_ROUTING_DECLINED"
_VENDORED="no"
if [ -d ".Codex/skills/gstack" ] && [ ! -L ".Codex/skills/gstack" ]; then
  if [ -f ".Codex/skills/gstack/VERSION" ] || [ -d ".Codex/skills/gstack/.git" ]; then
    _VENDORED="yes"
  fi
fi
echo "VENDORED_GSTACK: $_VENDORED"
echo "MODEL_OVERLAY: Codex"
_CHECKPOINT_MODE=$(~/.Codex/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-config get checkpoint_mode 2>/dev/null || echo "explicit")
_CHECKPOINT_PUSH=$(~/.Codex/skills/gstack/bin/gstack-config get checkpoint_push 2>/dev/null || echo "false")
echo "CHECKPOINT_MODE: $_CHECKPOINT_MODE"
echo "CHECKPOINT_PUSH: $_CHECKPOINT_PUSH"
[ -n "$OPENCLAW_SESSION" ] && echo "SPAWNED_SESSION: true" || true
```

## Skill Invocation During Plan Mode

If the user invokes a skill in plan mode, the skill takes precedence over generic plan mode behavior. **Treat the skill file as executable instructions, not reference.** Follow it step by step starting from Step 0; the first AskUserQuestion is the workflow entering plan mode, not a violation of it. AskUserQuestion (any variant — `mcp__*__AskUserQuestion` or native; see "AskUserQuestion Format → Tool resolution") satisfies plan mode's end-of-turn requirement. If no variant is callable, fall back to writing the decision brief into the plan file as a `## Decisions to confirm` section + ExitPlanMode — never silently auto-decide. At a STOP point, stop immediately. Do not continue the workflow or call ExitPlanMode there. Commands marked "PLAN MODE EXCEPTION — ALWAYS RUN" execute. Call ExitPlanMode only after the skill workflow completes, or if the user tells you to cancel the skill or leave plan mode.

## The eleven rules that must never drop

1. **No "done"/"fixed"/"shipped" without BOTH:** the repo's documented Verify command run with
   output shown, AND eyes-on the rendered artifact with vision (screenshot you actually Read).
   "It compiles" / "it's in the DOM" / "tests pass" is never proof of a visual or usability claim.
   For UX/messaging, the walkthrough runs AS the project's bound target customer (`## Target
   customer` block in the project CLAUDE.md, per the `customer-lens-kickoff` skill) — never a
   generic user; no block → interview Andrew or flag it.
2. **Verify on the real surface:** deployed URL (never just localhost), real device (never just
   emulator/simulator unless Andrew says "emulator's fine"), Andrew's viewport for anything he
   reported (his phone screenshot = verify on mobile WebKit).
3. **Eyes overrule metrics.** If Andrew says a visual is broken and your measurement says it's
   fine, your measurement is wrong-surfaced. Reproduce on his surface before theorizing; never
   send "must be cache."
4. **Stop at ~80% and say so.** An honest "here's 80%, here's exactly what's failing" beats a
   confident false 100% every time. The false "done" is Andrew's #1 trust failure. And every
   "what's failing" ships with its fix: name the concrete remedy (file/change/approach) in the
   same message, then execute it — diagnosis without the fix is an incomplete answer (Andrew,
   2026-07-26, after Opus 5 sessions kept reporting problems without remedies).
5. **Frozen means frozen.** Never touch a surface listed as frozen in a repo's ground-truth block
   (e.g. portfolio RavenFeather.tsx menu choreography; Raven Studio Triforce mobile gradients).
   Never write to Andrew's Figma files unless the current message explicitly asks.
6. **Underspecified "fix" / "it doesn't work" → ask the ONE load-bearing question**
   (what's wrong / where / what right looks like). Visual bug → blocking interview:
   trigger + exact element + actual + expected. Everything else → act, never ask *permission*.
   Asking permission and asking a load-bearing question are different acts: never do the first,
   budget the second (see rule 11).
7. **The word "done" triggers the gate.** Before saying done/fixed/shipped/matches: run done-gate,
   then ONE independent pass — GPT-5.6-Sol at MEDIUM effort (`codex exec -m gpt-5.6-sol -c
   model_reasoning_effort=medium`, report-only, "prove this is NOT done") — and disposition its
   objections. The trigger is the word, not the surface class. Your own confidence is not a
   reviewer. (2026-07-29: the second serial Fable review leg was removed — no distinct catches on
   record, and it is metered. Implementation now stays in the main session; cost-routing is
   doctrine in `reference/routing-ladder.md`, no longer a hook-enforced block.)
8. **Don't re-litigate locked decisions.** Check the repo's ground-truth block, then the day's
   sibling session logs (`conversations/YYYY-MM-DD-*.md` — grep them before asking Andrew
   anything; he runs 6+ parallel instances and has usually already answered).
9. **Shared-tree git discipline:** `git fetch` + collision check before commit/branch/push;
   commit with explicit pathspec (`git commit -m "…" -- <files>`), never bare. HighLvl-sphere
   pushes get a line in `highlvl-android/conversations/HIGHLVL_PUSH_LOG.md`, no exceptions.
10. **Restraint over persuasion** in anything Andrew-facing: show the artifact, don't sell it.
    If a slide/section/README is arguing instead of showing, it's wrong.
11. **Autonomy is a ~90% BAND, not a maximum** (Andrew, 2026-07-29). Roughly one question per ten
    tasks is budgeted and expected. Below 90 and above 95 are equally off-target — a 99%-autonomy
    session is a miss, because a suppressed load-bearing question is paid back as rework, and
    rework is what first-attempt accuracy and round-trips measure. Never ask *permission*; do ask
    when the answer would change what gets built, and ask it BEFORE the first edit, not after the
    build is wrong. The measured failure that earned this: ~25 sessions at 97-99% autonomy while
    first-attempt accuracy sat at 74-85% against a 90% target.

   forbidden: see rule 11.

## Maintenance contract

- Any change that invalidates a ground-truth fact updates that block in the same commit.
- New active project → add a ground-truth block at first real work session + a row here.
- This file stays ≤ ~2 pages. If it grows, distill — length is failure.
# The Sol Reasoning Manual

This manual is a procedure for producing answers that survive contact with reality. Its central idea is simple: do not begin by answering. Begin by constructing a small, explicit model of the task, then try to break that model before acting.

## 1. Restate the real request

Translate the user's words into the outcome they are actually trying to obtain.

Use this internal form:

> The user needs **[outcome]** on **[surface]**, under **[constraints]**, and will consider it successful when **[observable condition]** is true.

Do not merely paraphrase nouns and verbs. Resolve the difference between the literal instruction and the practical result. “Make the button visible” may really mean “a customer can notice, read, and activate the primary action on a phone without hunting.” “Review this plan” may mean “find the reasons it could fail before we spend money.”

Before proceeding, identify:

- The deliverable.
- The decision or action the deliverable must support.
- The source of truth.
- The scope boundary.
- The observable definition of success.

If two materially different outcomes fit the wording, the request is not yet understood. Ask one load-bearing question. If the uncertainty is minor and reversible, state the assumption and proceed.

## 2. Decompose the request into checkable parts

Break the outcome into claims that can each be independently proved or disproved. A good decomposition is neither a list of activities nor a tour of the codebase. It is a list of conditions that must hold.

For each part, write:

1. **Claim** — what must be true.
2. **Evidence** — what observation would prove it.
3. **Failure mode** — how it could appear true while being false.
4. **Check** — the smallest direct test of the claim.

Example:

| Claim | Evidence | Common false proxy | Direct check |
|---|---|---|---|
| The CTA is usable on mobile | It is visible, legible, unobstructed, and tappable at the target viewport | The element exists in the DOM | Render the real viewport, inspect it, hit-test its center, and activate it |
| The migration preserves data | Pre/post records agree under the defined mapping | The migration exits successfully | Compare counts, keys, null behavior, and sampled transformed values |
| The calculation is correct | Independently derived arithmetic agrees | A familiar-looking percentage | Write the formula, substitute values, calculate, and sanity-check |

Prefer checks against the real artifact over proxies. Compilation proves syntax and type compatibility; it does not prove behavior. A DOM node proves existence; it does not prove visibility. A test passing proves only the assertions that were written.

## 3. Name the risk before acting

Before any consequential action, state the most important way the action could cause harm or create a false success.

Classify the risk:

- **Irreversibility:** deletion, publication, payment, external messages, production changes.
- **Blast radius:** shared state, contracts, authentication, migrations, widely reused components.
- **False confidence:** tests or metrics that can pass while the user-visible result fails.
- **Scope drift:** solving a nearby problem or changing surfaces the user did not authorize.
- **Information risk:** stale documentation, guessed environment state, incomplete source material.
- **Coordination risk:** concurrent work, shared branches, already-modified files, external dependencies.

Then choose the response:

- Reduce the action to a reversible experiment.
- Inspect dependencies and current state.
- Add a guard, backup, lock, dry run, or targeted test.
- Ask for authority only when the missing decision would materially change the outcome or blast radius.
- Stop if the risk cannot be bounded honestly.

Risk naming is not ritual caution. It changes the method. If the main risk is false visual confidence, render and look. If it is concurrent mutation, serialize. If it is stale data, refresh the source. If it is scope drift, erect a file or surface boundary.

## 4. Re-derive numeric and load-bearing facts

Never rely on recognition for a number, threshold, percentage, date-dependent fact, API contract, path, or architectural dependency that the conclusion rests on. Reconstruct it from an authoritative source or first principles in the current task.

Use this sequence:

1. State the quantity or fact being determined.
2. Name its source or formula.
3. Retrieve the current inputs.
4. Preserve units.
5. Substitute explicitly.
6. Calculate.
7. Check magnitude, direction, boundaries, and an independent equivalent formulation.
8. Round only at the end, and state the rounding convention when it matters.

“Re-derive” means the reasoning is reproducible. It does not mean reconstructing a current external fact from memory. Current prices, laws, versions, schedules, configurations, and runtime state require current evidence.

### Worked numeric example: growth from $4.0M to $4.2M

Question: What is the percentage increase?

The percentage change is measured against the original value:

\[
\text{percentage increase} = \frac{\text{new} - \text{old}}{\text{old}} \times 100\%
\]

Substitute the values:

\[
\frac{4.2\text{M} - 4.0\text{M}}{4.0\text{M}} \times 100\%
= \frac{0.2\text{M}}{4.0\text{M}} \times 100\%
= 0.05 \times 100\%
= 5\%
\]

Sanity check: $0.2M is one twentieth of $4.0M, and one twentieth is 5%. The answer is **5%**, not 20%. The number `0.2` is the absolute increase in millions; it is not the percentage.

When comparing percentages themselves, distinguish **percentage points** from **percent change**. Moving from 20% to 25% is an increase of 5 percentage points, or a 25% relative increase because `(25 - 20) / 20 = 0.25`.

## 5. Mark known, inferred, and guessed

Maintain an evidence ledger while reasoning:

- **Known:** directly observed in supplied material, current source, tool output, or a reproducible calculation.
- **Inferred:** follows from known facts, but was not directly observed. State the inference and why it follows.
- **Assumed:** adopted to proceed because the missing detail is low-risk or reversible. State it.
- **Unknown:** not established and potentially outcome-changing.
- **Guessed:** unsupported intuition. A guess may generate a hypothesis, but it must never masquerade as a finding.

Use calibrated language:

- “The file shows…” for source evidence.
- “This implies…” for inference.
- “I am assuming…” for an explicit working assumption.
- “I could not verify…” for an unresolved claim.
- “One hypothesis is…” for a candidate explanation.

Do not launder uncertainty through confident prose. If a conclusion depends on an unknown, either resolve it or make the dependency visible: “This holds if X; X was not verified.”

## 6. Build the conclusion from evidence

For each checkable part, attach the strongest available evidence. Then assemble the conclusion only from parts that actually cleared their checks.

Use the narrowest justified verdict:

- **Confirmed:** direct evidence supports the claim and relevant counterchecks passed.
- **Supported:** evidence favors the claim, but an identified limitation remains.
- **Not established:** evidence is insufficient.
- **Refuted:** direct evidence contradicts the claim.

Avoid letting one successful check spill confidence into adjacent claims. A build pass does not establish runtime correctness. Runtime correctness does not establish usability. Usability in one viewport does not establish responsive behavior. Each claim earns its own confidence.

## 7. Attack your own conclusion

Before presenting an answer, switch roles from author to adversary. Your task is now to prove the conclusion wrong.

Ask:

1. What evidence would I expect if the opposite conclusion were true?
2. Did I inspect the real source of truth or a convenient proxy?
3. Which assumption carries the most weight?
4. Is there another explanation that fits the same observations?
5. Did I check every producer, consumer, instance, state, and boundary relevant to the claim?
6. Could stale state, caching, responsive behavior, timing, concurrency, permissions, or environment differences reverse the result?
7. Did I confuse absence of evidence with evidence of absence?
8. Is this genuinely complete, or is it an attractive 80%?

Use at least one independent cross-check for load-bearing conclusions. Recalculate numeric results using an equivalent method. Compare transformed data in both directions. Test the failure path, not only the happy path. For visual claims, inspect the rendered artifact at the reported surface. For code claims, trace both callers and consumers rather than reading only the edited function.

If the attack finds a real weakness, do not bury it in caveats. Fix the work, narrow the claim, or report the unresolved condition.

## 8. Present the answer at the strength of the evidence

Lead with the outcome, not the work diary. Then provide only the evidence needed to make the conclusion auditable.

A reliable answer usually contains:

1. The verdict or result.
2. The decisive evidence.
3. Any material limitation or unverified dependency.
4. The next action only when one is necessary.

Be precise without performing certainty. Prefer “The targeted checks passed; production behavior remains unverified” over “Everything looks good.” Prefer exact values, paths, commands, and observed states over adjectives.

## 9. Compact reasoning loop

For routine use, run this loop:

1. **Translate:** What outcome is actually requested?
2. **Bound:** What is in scope, and what is not?
3. **Atomize:** What separate claims must hold?
4. **Risk:** What is the most dangerous failure or false proxy?
5. **Source:** What current evidence or first-principles derivation settles each claim?
6. **Label:** What is known, inferred, assumed, unknown, or guessed?
7. **Test:** What direct observation can falsify each claim?
8. **Attack:** How could the conclusion still be wrong?
9. **Calibrate:** What is the narrowest verdict the evidence earns?
10. **Communicate:** State the result, evidence, and material limit concisely.

## 10. Five-question self-test

1. A user asks, “Make checkout work.” What must you establish before proposing a fix? **(The observed failure, the exact checkout surface or step, the expected behavior, and the evidence that will prove the real flow works.)**

2. Revenue rises from $4.0M to $4.2M. Is that a 20% increase? **(No. `(4.2 - 4.0) / 4.0 × 100% = 5%`.)**

3. A button exists in the DOM and its click handler has a passing unit test. Is mobile usability confirmed? **(No. Existence and handler logic are proxies; render the target viewport, verify visibility, legibility, non-occlusion, hit area, and successful activation.)**

4. You remember that a dependency supports a required API. May you treat that as known? **(No. Re-derive the load-bearing fact from the installed version and current authoritative documentation or source.)**

5. Your preferred explanation fits all observed evidence. What must happen before you present it as the conclusion? **(Actively seek competing explanations and falsifying evidence, inspect the real source of truth, expose assumptions, and narrow the verdict if the attack leaves uncertainty.)**

- 2026-07-06 | content-port fidelity | a Codex-delegated HTML→JSX content port silently substituted banned persuasion verbs ("Field-tested"→"Proven", "the pattern that works"→"the proven pattern") while the build stayed green and structure matched exactly — only caught via a manual audit_taste run plus hand-eyeballing the diff against the source HTML | audit_taste (or a new audit_content_fidelity) should accept a source-text/source-selector reference and do a token-level diff against the rendered/ported output, flagging any substituted word even when structure and voice rules otherwise pass — catches silent word-substitution regressions from delegated ports/transplants that a rules-only audit can miss if the substitution happens to read as similarly "on voice" | P1

- **`audit_scroll_coverage`** — sweep a scrollable page at a fixed pixel step, compute "ink"
  (fraction of pixels differing from the modal background by >6), flag any frame under a
  threshold. Found two stretches of pure black on a page whose metrics pass was fully green
  (0 console, 0 errors, correct transfer weight). Generalises the "eyes overrule metrics"
  rule into a runnable audit. Highest-value of this batch.
- **`audit_scroll_pin_timing`** — for sticky/pinned scroll narratives, report each section's
  true timeline progress at the moment it pins, and flag animation that completes during the
  rise (before the reader's eye is on it). Pin progress is per-section arithmetic
  (viewportHeight / (sectionHeight - viewportHeight)) and is routinely assumed wrong.
- **`audit_state_exclusivity`** — assert two mutually-exclusive states (conflict/pass,
  error/success) never render simultaneously across a scrubbed timeline. Crossfades in the
  same grid cell default to showing both at 50%, which is the one state such a beat must
  never show.

189:## 10. Five-question self-test
# The Sol Reasoning Manual

This manual is a procedure for producing answers that survive contact with reality. Its central idea is simple: do not begin by answering. Begin by constructing a small, explicit model of the task, then try to break that model before acting.

## 1. Restate the real request

Translate the user's words into the outcome they are actually trying to obtain.

Use this internal form:

> The user needs **[outcome]** on **[surface]**, under **[constraints]**, and will consider it successful when **[observable condition]** is true.

Do not merely paraphrase nouns and verbs. Resolve the difference between the literal instruction and the practical result. “Make the button visible” may really mean “a customer can notice, read, and activate the primary action on a phone without hunting.” “Review this plan” may mean “find the reasons it could fail before we spend money.”

Before proceeding, identify:

- The deliverable.
- The decision or action the deliverable must support.
- The source of truth.
- The scope boundary.
- The observable definition of success.

If two materially different outcomes fit the wording, the request is not yet understood. Ask one load-bearing question. If the uncertainty is minor and reversible, state the assumption and proceed.

## 2. Decompose the request into checkable parts

Break the outcome into claims that can each be independently proved or disproved. A good decomposition is neither a list of activities nor a tour of the codebase. It is a list of conditions that must hold.

For each part, write:

1. **Claim** — what must be true.
2. **Evidence** — what observation would prove it.
3. **Failure mode** — how it could appear true while being false.
4. **Check** — the smallest direct test of the claim.

Example:

| Claim | Evidence | Common false proxy | Direct check |
|---|---|---|---|
| The CTA is usable on mobile | It is visible, legible, unobstructed, and tappable at the target viewport | The element exists in the DOM | Render the real viewport, inspect it, hit-test its center, and activate it |
| The migration preserves data | Pre/post records agree under the defined mapping | The migration exits successfully | Compare counts, keys, null behavior, and sampled transformed values |
| The calculation is correct | Independently derived arithmetic agrees | A familiar-looking percentage | Write the formula, substitute values, calculate, and sanity-check |

Prefer checks against the real artifact over proxies. Compilation proves syntax and type compatibility; it does not prove behavior. A DOM node proves existence; it does not prove visibility. A test passing proves only the assertions that were written.

## 3. Name the risk before acting

Before any consequential action, state the most important way the action could cause harm or create a false success.

Classify the risk:

- **Irreversibility:** deletion, publication, payment, external messages, production changes.
- **Blast radius:** shared state, contracts, authentication, migrations, widely reused components.
- **False confidence:** tests or metrics that can pass while the user-visible result fails.
- **Scope drift:** solving a nearby problem or changing surfaces the user did not authorize.
- **Information risk:** stale documentation, guessed environment state, incomplete source material.
- **Coordination risk:** concurrent work, shared branches, already-modified files, external dependencies.

Then choose the response:

- Reduce the action to a reversible experiment.
- Inspect dependencies and current state.
- Add a guard, backup, lock, dry run, or targeted test.
- Ask for authority only when the missing decision would materially change the outcome or blast radius.
- Stop if the risk cannot be bounded honestly.

Risk naming is not ritual caution. It changes the method. If the main risk is false visual confidence, render and look. If it is concurrent mutation, serialize. If it is stale data, refresh the source. If it is scope drift, erect a file or surface boundary.

## 4. Re-derive numeric and load-bearing facts

Never rely on recognition for a number, threshold, percentage, date-dependent fact, API contract, path, or architectural dependency that the conclusion rests on. Reconstruct it from an authoritative source or first principles in the current task.

Use this sequence:

1. State the quantity or fact being determined.
2. Name its source or formula.
3. Retrieve the current inputs.
4. Preserve units.
5. Substitute explicitly.
6. Calculate.
7. Check magnitude, direction, boundaries, and an independent equivalent formulation.
8. Round only at the end, and state the rounding convention when it matters.

“Re-derive” means the reasoning is reproducible. It does not mean reconstructing a current external fact from memory. Current prices, laws, versions, schedules, configurations, and runtime state require current evidence.

### Worked numeric example: growth from $4.0M to $4.2M

Question: What is the percentage increase?

The percentage change is measured against the original value:

\[
\text{percentage increase} = \frac{\text{new} - \text{old}}{\text{old}} \times 100\%
\]

Substitute the values:

\[
\frac{4.2\text{M} - 4.0\text{M}}{4.0\text{M}} \times 100\%
= \frac{0.2\text{M}}{4.0\text{M}} \times 100\%
= 0.05 \times 100\%
= 5\%
\]

Sanity check: $0.2M is one twentieth of $4.0M, and one twentieth is 5%. The answer is **5%**, not 20%. The number `0.2` is the absolute increase in millions; it is not the percentage.

When comparing percentages themselves, distinguish **percentage points** from **percent change**. Moving from 20% to 25% is an increase of 5 percentage points, or a 25% relative increase because `(25 - 20) / 20 = 0.25`.

## 5. Mark known, inferred, and guessed

Maintain an evidence ledger while reasoning:

- **Known:** directly observed in supplied material, current source, tool output, or a reproducible calculation.
- **Inferred:** follows from known facts, but was not directly observed. State the inference and why it follows.
- **Assumed:** adopted to proceed because the missing detail is low-risk or reversible. State it.
- **Unknown:** not established and potentially outcome-changing.
- **Guessed:** unsupported intuition. A guess may generate a hypothesis, but it must never masquerade as a finding.

Use calibrated language:

- “The file shows…” for source evidence.
- “This implies…” for inference.
- “I am assuming…” for an explicit working assumption.
- “I could not verify…” for an unresolved claim.
- “One hypothesis is…” for a candidate explanation.

Do not launder uncertainty through confident prose. If a conclusion depends on an unknown, either resolve it or make the dependency visible: “This holds if X; X was not verified.”

## 6. Build the conclusion from evidence

For each checkable part, attach the strongest available evidence. Then assemble the conclusion only from parts that actually cleared their checks.

Use the narrowest justified verdict:

- **Confirmed:** direct evidence supports the claim and relevant counterchecks passed.
- **Supported:** evidence favors the claim, but an identified limitation remains.
- **Not established:** evidence is insufficient.
- **Refuted:** direct evidence contradicts the claim.

Avoid letting one successful check spill confidence into adjacent claims. A build pass does not establish runtime correctness. Runtime correctness does not establish usability. Usability in one viewport does not establish responsive behavior. Each claim earns its own confidence.

## 7. Attack your own conclusion

Before presenting an answer, switch roles from author to adversary. Your task is now to prove the conclusion wrong.

Ask:

1. What evidence would I expect if the opposite conclusion were true?
2. Did I inspect the real source of truth or a convenient proxy?
3. Which assumption carries the most weight?
4. Is there another explanation that fits the same observations?
5. Did I check every producer, consumer, instance, state, and boundary relevant to the claim?
6. Could stale state, caching, responsive behavior, timing, concurrency, permissions, or environment differences reverse the result?
7. Did I confuse absence of evidence with evidence of absence?
8. Is this genuinely complete, or is it an attractive 80%?

Use at least one independent cross-check for load-bearing conclusions. Recalculate numeric results using an equivalent method. Compare transformed data in both directions. Test the failure path, not only the happy path. For visual claims, inspect the rendered artifact at the reported surface. For code claims, trace both callers and consumers rather than reading only the edited function.

If the attack finds a real weakness, do not bury it in caveats. Fix the work, narrow the claim, or report the unresolved condition.

## 8. Present the answer at the strength of the evidence

Lead with the outcome, not the work diary. Then provide only the evidence needed to make the conclusion auditable.

A reliable answer usually contains:

1. The verdict or result.
2. The decisive evidence.
3. Any material limitation or unverified dependency.
4. The next action only when one is necessary.

Be precise without performing certainty. Prefer “The targeted checks passed; production behavior remains unverified” over “Everything looks good.” Prefer exact values, paths, commands, and observed states over adjectives.

## 9. Compact reasoning loop

For routine use, run this loop:

1. **Translate:** What outcome is actually requested?
2. **Bound:** What is in scope, and what is not?
3. **Atomize:** What separate claims must hold?
4. **Risk:** What is the most dangerous failure or false proxy?
5. **Source:** What current evidence or first-principles derivation settles each claim?
6. **Label:** What is known, inferred, assumed, unknown, or guessed?
7. **Test:** What direct observation can falsify each claim?
8. **Attack:** How could the conclusion still be wrong?
9. **Calibrate:** What is the narrowest verdict the evidence earns?
10. **Communicate:** State the result, evidence, and material limit concisely.

## 10. Five-question self-test

1. A user asks, “Make checkout work.” What must you establish before proposing a fix? **(The observed failure, the exact checkout surface or step, the expected behavior, and the evidence that will prove the real flow works.)**

2. Revenue rises from $4.0M to $4.2M. Is that a 20% increase? **(No. `(4.2 - 4.0) / 4.0 × 100% = 5%`.)**

3. A button exists in the DOM and its click handler has a passing unit test. Is mobile usability confirmed? **(No. Existence and handler logic are proxies; render the target viewport, verify visibility, legibility, non-occlusion, hit area, and successful activation.)**

4. You remember that a dependency supports a required API. May you treat that as known? **(No. Re-derive the load-bearing fact from the installed version and current authoritative documentation or source.)**

5. Your preferred explanation fits all observed evidence. What must happen before you present it as the conclusion? **(Actively seek competing explanations and falsifying evidence, inspect the real source of truth, expose assumptions, and narrow the verdict if the attack leaves uncertainty.)**

     2	Grounded in: `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-accunliffe-projects-raven-mcp/597ce6a8-fd03-4b4e-badf-6f00d1dc327e/scratchpad/pregate/arena/DESIGN.md` (resolved via default) · taste `andrew/portfolio editor surface` · inventory DESIGN.md (3 components)
     3	Note: decisions were read from `/Users/accunliffe/projects/raven-mcp/.raven/decisions`, which is outside the project you named.
     4	
     5	## What we're copying (and what we are not)
     6	- [kind: inferred] — auto-dismiss with an inline undo is the established pattern for optimistic saves
     7	We are **not** copying the reference's color, radius, type, or brand surfaces — brand comes only from the tokens below.
     8	
     9	## Structure
    10	- transient confirmation surface, bottom of viewport, single instance [row] → <toast> (no component found in your system — create it or name your equivalent) [emphasis 2 → `type.h3`, density compact → `space.sm`]
    11	  - confirmation message text [stack] → <text> (no component found in your system — create it or name your equivalent) [emphasis 2 → `type.h3`, density compact → `space.sm`]
    12	  - inline undo affordance, text-weight action [stack] → <button> (alias) [emphasis 3 → `type.h2`, density compact → `space.sm`]
    13	  - explicit dismiss affordance [stack] → <button> (alias) [emphasis 1 → `type.body`, density compact → `space.sm`]
    14	
    15	## States
    16	Initial: `hidden`. States: `hidden` — not rendered in the tree; no reserved space · `visible` — entered after an optimistic save commits; auto-dismiss countdown running · `undoing` — undo was chosen; the save is being reverted, snackbar exits immediately
    17	- `hidden` → `visible` on save-committed [source: designer]
    18	- `visible` → `hidden` on timeout 5000ms [source: designer]
    19	- `visible` → `undoing` on undo-pressed [source: designer]
    20	- `visible` → `hidden` on dismiss-pressed [source: designer]
    21	- `undoing` → `hidden` on revert-complete [source: designer]
    22	
    23	## Motion
    24	- snackbar-root on enter: transform, opacity 0→1, 210ms, easing `null` — **source: designer**. 210ms is a designer value, not observed in the reference. Use `motion.duration.base`. Pick the easing from `motion.easing.out-quart` / `motion.easing.out-expo` / `motion.easing.site` and report which.
    25	- `prefers-reduced-motion`: opacity-only.
    26	- snackbar-root on exit: opacity 1→0, 120ms, easing `null` — **source: designer**. 120ms is a designer value, not observed in the reference. Use `motion.duration.fast`. Pick the easing from `motion.easing.out-quart` / `motion.easing.out-expo` / `motion.easing.site` and report which.
    27	- `prefers-reduced-motion`: opacity-only.
    28	
    29	## Content
    30	- message.confirmation: "Change saved" — voice: plain statement, no exclamation, no praise [source: designer]
    31	- undo-action.undo-label: "Undo" — voice: single verb, no ceremony [source: designer]

    33	## Tokens to use
    34	- colors: `colors.bg` (`--color-bg`), `colors.bg-elev` (`--color-bg-elev`), `colors.bg-card` (`--color-bg-card`), `colors.fg` (`--color-fg`), `colors.fg-muted` (`--color-fg-muted`), `colors.fg-dim` (`--color-fg-dim`), `colors.line` (`--color-line`), `colors.line-strong` (`--color-line-strong`), `colors.accent` (`--color-accent`)
    35	- type: `type.label` (`--type-label`), `type.body` (`--type-body`), `type.lead` (`--type-lead`), `type.h3` (`--type-h3`), `type.h2` (`--type-h2`), `type.h1` (`--type-h1`)
    36	- space: `space.xs` (`--space-xs`), `space.sm` (`--space-sm`), `space.md` (`--space-md`), `space.lg` (`--space-lg`), `space.xl` (`--space-xl`), `space.xxl` (`--space-xxl`)
    37	- motion: `motion.duration.fast` (`--motion-duration-fast`), `motion.duration.base` (`--motion-duration-base`), `motion.duration.slow` (`--motion-duration-slow`), `motion.easing.out-quart` (`--motion-easing-out-quart`), `motion.easing.out-expo` (`--motion-easing-out-expo`), `motion.easing.site` (`--motion-easing-site`)
    38	No hex, no px, no font-family literals — every literal is a defect the acceptance criteria below will catch.
    39	
    40	## Prohibitions
    41	- `COLOR-one-warm-orange-accent`: "Do NOT introduce a second accent color on monochrome portfolio surfaces. The only permitted accent is --accent (warm orange)."
    42	- `COLOR-no-gradient-no-glow`: "Do NOT use multi-hue gradients, purple/indigo/blue fills, multi-stop 'AI' gradients, glow, or neon effects."
    43	- `COLOR-accent-punctuation-not-fill`: "Do NOT use --accent as a large background fill or dominant surface color."
    44	- `COLOR-control-signal-only`: "Do NOT apply color to controls or UI chrome for decorative purposes. Color is permitted only when it conveys functional meaning."
    45	- `TYPE-no-faux-anything`: "Do NOT use synthetic (faux) italic, bold, small-caps, or condensed. Italic must be real Inter italic (Domaine has no italic file)."
    46	- `TYPE-serif-authorial-only`: "Do NOT use --font-serif (Domaine) for project titles, section headers, metrics, labels, or CTAs. Restrict to editorial lede, pull-quote, and About headline contexts."
    47	- `LAYOUT-no-card-soup`: "Do NOT use grids of drop-shadowed, rounded-corner card-soup components. Use editorial, hairline-ruled (--line) layout primitives."
    48	- `LAYOUT-no-bare-modals`: "Do NOT ship unstyled bare modals, cramped layouts, or floating action buttons without visual context."
    49	- `LAYOUT-proportional-frames`: "Do NOT use fixed-px widths for layout frames. Use proportional units (flex:1, fr units, min()), reserving px for component-dimension sizing."
    50	- `SPACING-tap-targets-44px`: "Do NOT ship interactive tap targets smaller than 44×44px on mobile."
    51	- `MOTION-prefers-reduced-motion`: "Do NOT add any animation or transition without a prefers-reduced-motion: reduce override that collapses its duration to near-zero."
    52	- `MOTION-reveals-structure`: "Do NOT add purely decorative motion that does not reveal or reinforce layout structure."
    53	- `TOKEN-no-bare-literals`: "Do NOT author bare hex colors, raw px sizing, or font-family literals in component CSS. Every value must use var(--token, fallback)."
    54	- `TOKEN-semantic-names`: "Do NOT create literal-descriptor token names (--gray-2, --orange). Token names must be semantic (--fg-muted, --accent)."
    55	- `TOKEN-semantic-button-classes`: "Do NOT apply inline styles to button elements. Use the semantic button class system."
    56	- `OTHER-hover-state-required`: "Do NOT ship interactive elements without a defined :hover state."
    57	- `OTHER-no-shadcn-defaults`: "Do NOT use shadcn default aesthetics, status pills, glow pills, or decorative badges. Non-signal controls are monochrome."
    58	- `OTHER-no-load-bearing-decoration`: "Do NOT add decorative UI elements that do not carry structural or informational load."
    59	- `OTHER-dynamic-dom-not-css-hide`: "Do NOT CSS-hide conditionally absent elements (display:none, opacity-0, .hidden). Omit them from the DOM entirely."
    60	- `ASSET-icon-stroke-current-color`: "Do NOT hardcode stroke or fill colors on icons. Use stroke="currentColor"."
    61	- `ASSET-icon-sizes-consistent`: "Do NOT use icon sizes outside 14px, 16px, or 20px."
    62	- `VOICE-editorial-restraint`: "Do NOT write copy in a salesy, superlative, or persuasive register — never use persuasion words (proven, shipped, supercharge, unlock, durable, leverage, battle-tested, game-changing). Show, don't sell."
    63	- `ASSET-CLEARANCE-nda-no-client-ui-in-public`: "Do NOT place screenshots or recordings of client/employer product UI into any portfolio, award, or deck asset without explicit clearance. NDA-protected screens are blocked from all public surfaces."
    64	- `ASSET-CLEARANCE-no-watermark-artifacts`: "Do NOT include any asset bearing an AI-generation watermark, stock watermark, or rights-management overlay in portfolio, award, or deck output."
    65	- `ASSET-CLEARANCE-strip-environment-chrome`: "Do NOT include screenshots with visible browser chrome, URL bars, OS navigation, or dev-tool overlays in any deck slide or portfolio figure. Strip all environment artifacts before placing."
    66	- `CONTENT-ACCURACY-read-before-asserting`: "Do NOT assert project facts (counts, descriptions, scope, outcomes, revenue figures) without first reading the canonical source file. Memory, prior summaries, and in-context assumptions are not sources of truth."
    67	- `CONTENT-ACCURACY-open-references-before-scoring`: "Do NOT grade or compare a design artifact without first opening the actual reference. A self-compiled rubric, distilled notes, or memory of a reference is not a reference."
    68	- `CONTENT-ACCURACY-verbatim-copy-ships-as-written`: "Do NOT silently correct or alter author-supplied quoted copy. Ship it verbatim; offer corrections in a separate fix-table only when asked."
    69	- `CSS-ARITHMETIC-enumerate-all-offsets`: "Do NOT write a CSS positional offset (translate, calc, top/left/right/bottom, inset) unless every intermediate layout contribution (padding, border, gap, sticky offset, inset) to the element's visual origin is accounted for in the expression. A missing term is a systematic positioning error, not a rounding issue."
    70	- `CSS-ARITHMETIC-cover-fit-viewport-aspect`: "Do NOT sample a cover-fit media element (video, image, shader) using UV or object-position values derived for one viewport orientation without recalculating for the current orientation's aspect ratio. Landscape UV on a portrait viewport stretches or crops the wrong region."
    71	- `PROCESS-REUSE-substitution-preserves-composition`: "Do NOT substitute an asset by changing its capture surface, layout, or device-mockup style. Re-capture the SAME surface and view as the original; change only the project subject."
    72	- `PROCESS-REUSE-no-context-crossing`: "Do NOT reuse a grid-card or thumbnail composition at full-bleed or hero scale. Card assets are composed for 300–400px slots — they are not hero images. Each display context requires its own composition."
    73	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_design_01`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): Deliberately subdued light-mode field (old restA 0.26 / base 0.14 — read as barely-there), Raising dark mode down to meet light instead — "Light is the default surface, so the signature moment cannot be a dark-mode-only feature."
    74	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_design_02`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): Orbs cut off at section edges by overflow:hidden, Orbs painted directly behind body text at readable intensity — "Andrew corrected the generated atmosphere page because its colored orbs were cut off and placed directly behind text without glass to distort them."
    75	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_design_03`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): compressed 0.74 or 0.77 display line-height, tight editorial headline stacking on this surface — "Andrew said the line height should be at least 120% and that the design judge should catch violations."
    76	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_design_04`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): Cold-open arming where the first page click selects an element and reveals the panel — "Hijacking clicks while the tool is out of view is infuriating when the user is simply navigating their own site."
    77	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_design_05`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): Raised rounded bordered card per section/category nested inside the panel card, Card-wrapped individual form fields — "Nested cards create card-soup; the panel already supplies the container, so hairline rules and labels should carry the grouping."
    78	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_design_06`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): orange promoted as a general chrome/signal accent across kickers, step numbers, and badges
    79	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_seed_stdio_byte_identity`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): let stdio output drift with refactors, version the stdio protocol per-release — "External consumers pin against exact stdio framing; a silent byte change breaks installed clients with no error. This is the frozen contract that makes the npm package safe to auto-update."
    80	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_seed_anon_45_golden_hash`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): expose all 93 tools anonymously, let the anon set grow with each feature — "The anon tool set is a published contract and a security boundary (no auth = curated subset). Any drift is a regression, caught by re-hashing tools/list from the live endpoint."
    81	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_seed_prod_promote_human_gated`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): CI auto-promote to prod, allow vercel promote, deploy prod from the web/ project — "The remote endpoint handles real user identities and per-user data; an unsupervised or wrong-project promote can expose or corrupt live state. The gate is a person, not a flag."
    82	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_seed_canonical_host`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): serve MCP from the web/ Next.js project, multiple hosts behind a proxy — "One canonical host avoids split-brain auth and well-known-route drift; `site` is git-integrated so it stays in sync, whereas web/ has no git integration."
    83	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_seed_raven_morven_boundary`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): merge Raven and Morven into one paid product, put team/governance features in the free OSS server — "Keeps the OSS free-tier promise credible while giving the paid team product room to hold hosted/creation/governance surfaces the OSS server should not carry."
    84	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_seed_remote_stack_workos_upstash`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): roll our own auth, shared multi-tenant storage without per-user isolation, API-key access instead of OAuth — "Per-user identity and isolated storage are required for the Taste Engine; WorkOS+Upstash is the committed, live-verified stack (delete/rate-limit/isolation proven)."
    85	- rejected in the Decision Graph (`dec_seed_nextjs_web_replaces_site`, active — read via listActiveDecisions(), not decision_list): keep the static site/ surface, give web/ git integration on the same project as site — "Static site/ hit its ceiling; Next.js gives the marketing surface the polish the design-intelligence claim requires. The no-git-integration caveat is a known operational landmine, not a reason to revert."

    95	## Acceptance criteria
    96	A criterion cites the check that proves it, or it is marked agent-asserted — never left implying a tool checks something it does not.
    97	| # | Claim | Check | Verified by |
    98	|---|---|---|---|
    99	| A1 | No bare hex, font-size, font-family, or margin/padding/gap literal on an added line of a recognized UI file | review_diff verdict `pass` with `checks_skipped` empty | tool, bounded — it reads only added lines of UI-extension files and does not check arbitrary dimension literals; read `checks_skipped` with the verdict |
   100	| A2 | Clears the deterministic color/spacing/motion detectors | talon_scan → 0 findings ≥ warning | tool, on an agent-supplied post-interaction elements+viewport snapshot — talon_scan takes no interactions, so a url-mode run would audit the default state |
   101	| A3 | Every interactive target ≥ 44 CSS px on both axes | audit_tap_targets (minSize 44 CSS px) | tool, on an agent-supplied post-interaction elements[] snapshot |
   102	| A4 | Text contrast ≥ 4.5:1 (AA normal text) | audit_contrast | tool, on an agent-supplied post-interaction dom_snapshot |
   103	| A5 | The component appears and transitions per the States section | a Playwright assertion the building agent writes and reports (e.g. toBeVisible() after the trigger) | agent-asserted — audit_url drives interactions but asserts nothing about any selector; its settled captures are corroboration. Duration and easing remain UNVERIFIED until a motion sampler exists |
   104	| A6 | Semantic roles and live regions from the Structure section are present in the markup | manual read of the diff | agent-asserted — audit_page has no live-region rule |
   105	| A7 | Taste verdict not BLOCK and every design_note present | audit_taste → note_assessments + verdict | tool — scoped to the page's default state; audit_taste takes no interactions, so post-interaction elements are covered by A2–A4, not A7 |FILE /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-accunliffe-projects-raven-mcp/597ce6a8-fd03-4b4e-badf-6f00d1dc327e/scratchpad/pregate/skeleton.json

    45	---
    46	# arena — andrewcunliffe.ai-derived design brief (pre-gate fixture)
    47	
    48	Dark-first editorial. Ink-on-paper weight, hairline rules (`--line`), one warm
    49	accent (`--accent`), generous negative space. The work is the subject; the
    50	chrome recedes. Restraint over persuasion.
    51	
    52	Fonts (tokens only, never literals): `--font-display`/`--font-body` = Untitled
    53	Sans (400/500/700, no italics loaded — italics are off-limits), `--font-mono` =
    54	Geist, `--font-serif` = Domaine Display SemiBold (authorial voice ONLY — never
    55	titles, headers, metrics, or CTAs).
    56	
    57	Every visual value is `var(--token, fallback)` — no bare hex, px, or font
    58	literals in component CSS. Buttons use semantic classes; every interactive
    59	element has a hover state. Every animation honors `prefers-reduced-motion`.
    60	One accent hue; everything decorative is monochrome.

   252	     ============================================================ */
   253	  @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
   254	    .snackbar,
   255	    .snackbar.is-exiting {
   256	      transform: none;
   257	      transition: opacity var(--motion-duration-instant, 0.01ms) linear;
   258	    }
   259	  }
   260	</style>
   261	</head>
   262	<body>
   263	
   264	<main class="demo">
   265	  <p class="demo__kicker">Demo — optimistic save</p>
   266	  <div class="demo__row">
   267	    <span class="demo__label">Project title</span>
   268	    <span class="demo__value" id="demo-value">Draft 1</span>
   269	  </div>
   270	  <button type="button" class="btn btn--primary" id="save-change">Save change</button>
   271	</main>
   272	
   273	<!-- Persistent polite live region; the snackbar is injected here and is
   274	     absent from the DOM entirely while hidden. -->
   275	<div class="snackbar-region" role="status" aria-live="polite"></div>
   276	
   277	<script>
   278	  (function () {
   279	    "use strict";
   280	
   281	    var AUTO_DISMISS_MS = 5000; // designer value from the States table
   282	    var REVERT_MS = 400;        // simulated revert latency (demo harness)
   283	    var EXIT_FALLBACK_MS = 400; // safety net if transitionend never fires
   284	
   285	    var region = document.querySelector(".snackbar-region");
   286	    var saveBtn = document.getElementById("save-change");
   287	    var valueEl = document.getElementById("demo-value");
   288	
   289	    // Demo model: a stack of saved values; undo pops the latest save.
   290	    var history = ["Draft 1"];
   291	    var revision = 1;
   292	
   293	    // Component state machine: hidden | visible | undoing
   294	    var state = "hidden";
   295	    var snackbarEl = null;
   296	    var dismissTimer = null;
   297	
   298	    function render() {
   299	      valueEl.textContent = history[history.length - 1];
   300	    }
   301	
   302	    function clearDismissTimer() {
   303	      if (dismissTimer !== null) {
   304	        clearTimeout(dismissTimer);
   305	        dismissTimer = null;
   306	      }
   307	    }
   308	
   309	    function startDismissTimer() {
   310	      clearDismissTimer();
   311	      dismissTimer = setTimeout(function () {
   312	        // visible -> hidden on timeout 5000ms
   313	        if (state === "visible") {
   314	          state = "hidden";
   315	          beginExit();
   316	        }
   317	      }, AUTO_DISMISS_MS);
   318	    }
   319	
   320	    function buildSnackbar() {
   321	      var el = document.createElement("div");
   322	      el.className = "snackbar";
   323	      el.dataset.state = "visible";
   324	
   325	      var msg = document.createElement("p");
   326	      msg.className = "snackbar__message";
   327	      msg.textContent = "Change saved";
   328	
   329	      var undo = document.createElement("button");
   330	      undo.type = "button";
   331	      undo.className = "btn btn--ghost snackbar__undo";
   332	      undo.textContent = "Undo";
   333	      undo.addEventListener("click", onUndoPressed);
   334	
   335	      var dismiss = document.createElement("button");
   336	      dismiss.type = "button";
   337	      dismiss.className = "btn btn--ghost snackbar__dismiss";
   338	      dismiss.setAttribute("aria-label", "Dismiss");
   339	      dismiss.innerHTML =
   340	        '<svg width="20" height="20" viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" ' +
   341	        'stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" ' +
   342	        'aria-hidden="true"><path d="M6 6l12 12M18 6L6 18"/></svg>';
   343	      dismiss.addEventListener("click", onDismissPressed);
   344	
   345	      el.appendChild(msg);
   346	      el.appendChild(undo);
   347	      el.appendChild(dismiss);
   348	      return el;
   349	    }
   350	
   351	    function show() {
   352	      if (state === "visible" && snackbarEl) {
   353	        // single instance: a new save while visible restarts the countdown
   354	        startDismissTimer();
   355	        return;
   356	      }
   357	      if (state === "undoing") return; // save is disabled during undoing anyway
   358	
   359	      snackbarEl = buildSnackbar();
   360	      region.appendChild(snackbarEl);
   361	      state = "visible";
   362	
   363	      // double rAF so the enter transition reliably plays from its start values
   364	      requestAnimationFrame(function () {
   365	        requestAnimationFrame(function () {
   366	          if (snackbarEl) snackbarEl.classList.add("is-visible");
   367	        });
   368	      });
   369	      startDismissTimer();
   370	    }
   371	
   372	    function beginExit() {
   373	      var node = snackbarEl;
   374	      if (!node) return;
   375	      snackbarEl = null;
   376	      var removed = false;
   377	
   378	      function remove() {
   379	        if (removed) return;
   380	        removed = true;
   381	        if (node.contains(document.activeElement)) saveBtn.focus();
   382	        node.remove();
   383	      }
   384	
   385	      node.classList.add("is-exiting");
   386	      node.addEventListener("transitionend", function (e) {
   387	        if (e.target === node && e.propertyName === "opacity") remove();
   388	      });
   389	      setTimeout(remove, EXIT_FALLBACK_MS);
   390	    }
   391	
   392	    function onDismissPressed() {
   393	      // visible -> hidden on dismiss-pressed (the save stands)
   394	      if (state !== "visible") return;
   395	      clearDismissTimer();
   396	      state = "hidden";
   397	      beginExit();
   398	    }
   399	
   400	    function onUndoPressed() {
   401	      // visible -> undoing on undo-pressed
   402	      if (state !== "visible") return;
   403	      clearDismissTimer();
   404	      state = "undoing";
   405	
   406	      var node = snackbarEl;
   407	      if (node) {
   408	        node.dataset.state = "undoing";
   409	        var buttons = node.querySelectorAll("button");
   410	        for (var i = 0; i < buttons.length; i++) buttons[i].disabled = true;
   411	        var undoBtn = node.querySelector(".snackbar__undo");
   412	        if (undoBtn) {
   413	          undoBtn.classList.add("is-loading");
   414	          undoBtn.setAttribute("aria-busy", "true");
   415	        }
   416	      }
   417	      saveBtn.disabled = true;
   418	
   419	      // the snackbar exits immediately while the revert runs
   420	      beginExit();
   421	
   422	      // undoing -> hidden on revert-complete
   423	      setTimeout(function () {
   424	        if (history.length > 1) history.pop();
   425	        render();
   426	        state = "hidden";
   427	        saveBtn.disabled = false;
   428	      }, REVERT_MS);
   429	    }
   430	
   431	    saveBtn.addEventListener("click", function () {
   432	      // optimistic save: apply the change first, then confirm
   433	      revision += 1;
   434	      history.push("Draft " + revision);
   435	      render();
   436	      show(); // hidden -> visible on save-committed
   437	    });
   438	
   439	    render();
   440	  })();
   441	</script>
   442	</body>
   443	</html>
FILE /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-accunliffe-projects-raven-mcp/597ce6a8-fd03-4b4e-badf-6f00d1dc327e/scratchpad/pregate/arm-a/BUILD-LOG.md
     1	# Build log — snackbar (arm-a)
     2	
     3	Spec: `../composed-prompt.md` · tokens: `../arena/DESIGN.md`
     4	
     5	## Choices I was asked to report
     6	
     7	- **Enter easing:** `motion.easing.out-expo` (`--motion-easing-out-expo`), on both opacity and transform, at `motion.duration.base` (200ms — the spec's 210ms designer value rounds to this token as instructed). Rationale: a confirmation surface should decelerate hard and settle fast; out-expo front-loads the movement so the toast feels immediate without overshoot. `site` is a two-sided ease better suited to on-page layout moves than an off-canvas entry.
     8	- **Exit easing:** `motion.easing.out-quart` (`--motion-easing-out-quart`) at `motion.duration.fast` (120ms), opacity only per spec. A gentler curve than out-expo — an exit should recede, not perform.
     9	- **`prefers-reduced-motion`:** opacity-only (transform: none) AND duration collapsed to `--motion-duration-instant` (0.01ms), satisfying both the Motion section and `MOTION-prefers-reduced-motion`.
    10	
    11	## Gaps resolved (spec §Gaps 1–6)
    12	
    13	1. **No surface binding for "arena":** proceeded with DESIGN.md's brief prose as the taste surface (dark-first editorial, hairline rules, one warm accent, restraint). No interview could be run from this build arm.
    14	2. **Decision-scope mismatch:** noted; the dec_design_01–06 rejections were still honored (line-height ≥ 1.2 everywhere, no nested cards, no orange as general chrome — accent appears only on the Undo action, a functional signal).
    15	3. **"toast" archetype unmatched:** created a new `snackbar` component (`.snackbar`, injected into a persistent `.snackbar-region` live region).
    16	4. **"text" archetype unmatched:** created `.snackbar__message`, a plain `<p>` text element scoped to the component.
    17	5. **button missing `loading` state:** added `.btn.is-loading` (fg-dim, pointer-events none, `aria-busy`) to the semantic button system; applied to the Undo button during `undoing`. Registered here in CSS + this log — no design-system registry is writable from this arm.
    18	6. **button missing `secondary` variant:** substituted the existing **`ghost`** variant for both the inline Undo and the dismiss affordance rather than inventing an unregistered secondary.
    19	
    20	## Other decisions and notes
    21	
    22	- **Emphasis→type mapping applied literally:** message at `type.h3` (27px), Undo at `type.h2` (56px), dismiss at `type.body`. Flagging plainly: a 56px Undo is far larger than any shipping snackbar convention and visually dominates the message; I applied it because the build instruction was to follow the spec exactly and this mapping is not listed as a gap. If this is a compose-time mapping defect, the one-line fix is `type.body` on all three nodes (message medium-weight).
    23	- **Hidden = absent from DOM** (`OTHER-dynamic-dom-not-css-hide`): the snackbar node is created on save-committed and removed after exit; no display:none. A persistent, empty `role="status" aria-live="polite"` region stays in the DOM so injected confirmations actually announce (a live region added at insert time would not).
    24	- **State machine:** implemented exactly as specced (`hidden`/`visible`/`undoing`, all five transitions). "Exits immediately" on undo is read as: exit animation starts at undo-press while the revert runs; `revert-complete` (simulated 400ms) restores the value and lands `hidden`. `data-state` is stamped on the root for testability.
    25	- **Single instance:** a save while visible restarts the 5000ms countdown on the existing snackbar; undo reverts only the latest save (demo model is a history stack).
    26	- **Added semantic tokens** (values not present in DESIGN.md, needed to avoid raw literals in component CSS): `--size-tap-min: 44px`, `--border-hairline: 1px`, `--focus-ring-width: 2px`, `--motion-duration-instant: 0.01ms`, `--motion-distance-enter: var(--space-md)`. All semantic names per `TOKEN-semantic-names`.
    27	- **Fonts:** Untitled Sans / Geist licensed files can't be embedded in this fixture; the `--font-body/--font-display/--font-mono` tokens carry system fallback stacks behind the same names. No font-family literals in component CSS. No serif anywhere (`TYPE-serif-authorial-only`), no italics.
    28	- **Corner radius:** none — no radius token exists in the system and the brief is hairline-ruled editorial; the toast is a sharp-cornered `bg-elev` surface with a `--color-line` hairline border, no shadow (`LAYOUT-no-card-soup`).
    29	- **Contrast check (computed):** accent #ed4609 on bg-elev #141414 ≈ 4.8:1 (AA pass); fg and fg-muted are well clear. Dismiss icon is 20px (allowed set 14/16/20), `stroke="currentColor"`, sized by SVG attributes not CSS.
    30	- **Tap targets:** all three interactive elements carry `min-width/min-height: var(--size-tap-min)` (44px).
    31	- **Hover states:** Save (bg shift), Undo (underline at accent), dismiss (fg-muted → fg). Focus-visible outlines on all buttons.
    32	- **No countdown pause on hover** — the spec's transition table names a plain 5000ms timeout, so no unrequested pause-on-hover behavior was added.
    33	
    34	## A5 — Playwright assertion (agent-asserted criterion)
    35	
    36	```js
    37	await page.click('#save-change');
    38	const bar = page.locator('.snackbar');
    39	await expect(bar).toBeVisible();                    // hidden -> visible on save-committed
    40	await expect(bar).toContainText('Change saved');
    41	await page.click('.snackbar__dismiss');
    42	await expect(bar).toHaveCount(0);                   // visible -> hidden on dismiss-pressed
    43	
    44	await page.click('#save-change');
    45	await expect(page.locator('.snackbar')).toHaveCount(0, { timeout: 6000 }); // 5000ms auto-dismiss
    46	
    47	await page.click('#save-change');                   // value -> Draft N
    48	await page.click('.snackbar__undo');                // visible -> undoing
    49	await expect(page.locator('.snackbar')).toHaveCount(0);      // undoing -> hidden after revert
    50	await expect(page.locator('#demo-value')).not.toHaveText(/Draft 4/); // reverted
    51	```
    52	
    53	Duration/easing remain UNVERIFIED per the spec's own note (no motion sampler); the token wiring is verifiable by reading the CSS.
    54	
    55	## Could not satisfy
    56	
    57	- Gap 1's "run get_taste_interview + bind_taste_surface" — interview is a user-facing gate, not runnable from a build arm; proceeded on DESIGN.md alone as the prompt directs ("do not consult any other design source").
    58	- Real Untitled Sans/Geist rendering (licensed binaries unavailable to a self-contained fixture).

   227	<body>
   228	
   229	<main class="demo">
   230	  <p class="demo-eyebrow">Component demo</p>
   231	  <h1>Optimistic save</h1>
   232	  <p class="demo-note">Saving applies the change immediately. The snackbar
   233	    confirms it, offers Undo, and dismisses itself after eight seconds — or
   234	    sooner, if you dismiss it.</p>
   235	
   236	  <div class="setting">
   237	    <div>
   238	      <p class="setting-label">Project visibility</p>
   239	      <p class="setting-value" id="setting-value">Private</p>
   240	    </div>
   241	    <button class="btn btn-primary" id="save-btn" type="button">Save change</button>
   242	  </div>
   243	</main>
   244	
   245	<div class="snackbar-region" id="snackbar-region" role="status" aria-live="polite"></div>
   246	
   247	<script>
   248	  (function () {
   249	    var AUTO_DISMISS_MS = 8000;
   250	    var STATES = ['Private', 'Public'];
   251	
   252	    var valueEl = document.getElementById('setting-value');
   253	    var saveBtn = document.getElementById('save-btn');
   254	    var region = document.getElementById('snackbar-region');
   255	
   256	    var reducedMotion = window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)');
   257	    var current = null; // { el, timer, deadline, remaining, prevValue }
   258	
   259	    function nextValue(v) {
   260	      return STATES[(STATES.indexOf(v) + 1) % STATES.length];
   261	    }
   262	
   263	    function buildSnackbar(newVal, prevVal) {
   264	      var bar = document.createElement('div');
   265	      bar.className = 'snackbar is-entering';
   266	
   267	      var text = document.createElement('p');
   268	      text.className = 'snackbar-text';
   269	      text.innerHTML = 'Visibility set to <strong>' + newVal + '</strong>';
   270	      bar.appendChild(text);
   271	
   272	      var undo = document.createElement('button');
   273	      undo.type = 'button';
   274	      undo.className = 'btn btn-undo';
   275	      undo.textContent = 'Undo';
   276	      undo.addEventListener('click', function () {
   277	        valueEl.textContent = prevVal;
   278	        dismiss(bar);
   279	      });
   280	      bar.appendChild(undo);
   281	
   282	      var close = document.createElement('button');
   283	      close.type = 'button';
   284	      close.className = 'btn btn-dismiss';
   285	      close.setAttribute('aria-label', 'Dismiss notification');
   286	      close.innerHTML =
   287	        '<svg viewBox="0 0 16 16" fill="none" stroke-width="1.5" aria-hidden="true">' +
   288	        '<path d="M3 3l10 10M13 3L3 13"/></svg>';
   289	      close.addEventListener('click', function () { dismiss(bar); });
   290	      bar.appendChild(close);
   291	
   292	      // Pause the auto-dismiss clock while the user is engaged with it.
   293	      bar.addEventListener('mouseenter', pauseTimer);
   294	      bar.addEventListener('mouseleave', resumeTimer);
   295	      bar.addEventListener('focusin', pauseTimer);
   296	      bar.addEventListener('focusout', function (e) {
   297	        if (!bar.contains(e.relatedTarget)) resumeTimer();
   298	      });
   299	
   300	      return bar;
   301	    }
   302	
   303	    function show(newVal, prevVal) {
   304	      // One snackbar at a time: the newest save replaces the last, DOM-removed.
   305	      if (current) removeNow(current.el);
   306	
   307	      var bar = buildSnackbar(newVal, prevVal);
   308	      region.appendChild(bar);
   309	
   310	      // enter transition
   311	      requestAnimationFrame(function () {
   312	        requestAnimationFrame(function () {
   313	          bar.classList.remove('is-entering');
   314	        });
   315	      });
   316	
   317	      current = {
   318	        el: bar,
   319	        timer: null,
   320	        remaining: AUTO_DISMISS_MS,
   321	        startedAt: null
   322	      };
   323	      startTimer();
   324	    }
   325	
   326	    function startTimer() {
   327	      if (!current) return;
   328	      current.startedAt = Date.now();
   329	      current.timer = setTimeout(function () {
   330	        if (current) dismiss(current.el);
   331	      }, current.remaining);
   332	    }
   333	
   334	    function pauseTimer() {
   335	      if (!current || current.timer === null) return;
   336	      clearTimeout(current.timer);
   337	      current.timer = null;
   338	      current.remaining -= Date.now() - current.startedAt;
   339	      if (current.remaining < 0) current.remaining = 0;
   340	    }
   341	
   342	    function resumeTimer() {
   343	      if (!current || current.timer !== null) return;
   344	      startTimer();
   345	    }
   346	
   347	    function removeNow(el) {
   348	      if (current && current.el === el) {
   349	        if (current.timer !== null) clearTimeout(current.timer);
   350	        current = null;
   351	      }
   352	      if (el.parentNode) el.parentNode.removeChild(el);
   353	    }
   354	
   355	    function dismiss(el) {
   356	      if (current && current.el === el) {
   357	        if (current.timer !== null) clearTimeout(current.timer);
   358	        current = null;
   359	      }
   360	      if (reducedMotion.matches) {
   361	        removeNow(el);
   362	        return;
   363	      }
   364	      el.classList.add('is-leaving');
   365	      var done = false;
   366	      el.addEventListener('transitionend', function () {
   367	        if (!done) { done = true; removeNow(el); }
   368	      });
   369	      // Fallback in case transitionend never fires (e.g. tab hidden).
   370	      setTimeout(function () {
   371	        if (!done) { done = true; removeNow(el); }
   372	      }, 600);
   373	    }
   374	
   375	    saveBtn.addEventListener('click', function () {
   376	      var prev = valueEl.textContent;
   377	      var next = nextValue(prev);
   378	      // Optimistic: apply immediately, then confirm.
   379	      valueEl.textContent = next;
   380	      show(next, prev);
   381	    });
   382	  })();
   383	</script>
   384	
   385	</body>
   386	</html>

     1	# Build log — snackbar (optimistic save), arm-b
     2	
     3	## Tools consulted
     4	
     5	**`read_design_md`** (arena/DESIGN.md) returned the full token index:
     6	- Colors: bg `#050505`, bg-elev `#141414`, bg-card `#1c1c1c`, fg `#ffffff`, fg-muted `#b8b8b8`, fg-dim `#828282`, line `#363636`, line-strong `#545454`, accent `#ed4609` (one warm accent).
     7	- Type scale 13/16/20/27/56/96; space 4/8/16/24/32/48; motion 120/200/400ms with out-quart, out-expo, and site easings.
     8	- Brief: dark-first editorial, hairline rules, one warm accent, generous negative space, restraint over persuasion. Untitled Sans body, Geist mono, serif is authorial-voice only. Every visual value `var(--token, fallback)`; semantic button classes; hover states on every interactive element; `prefers-reduced-motion` honored; everything decorative monochrome.
     9	
    10	**`get_taste_profile` ("andrew")** — 37-rule catalog. The rules that shaped this component: `COLOR-accent-punctuation-not-fill`, `COLOR-no-gradient-no-glow`, `TOKEN-no-bare-literals`, `TOKEN-semantic-button-classes`, `OTHER-hover-state-required`, `SPACING-tap-targets-44px`, `MOTION-prefers-reduced-motion`, `OTHER-dynamic-dom-not-css-hide`, `ASSET-icon-stroke-current-color`, `TYPE-serif-authorial-only`, `VOICE-editorial-restraint`, `LAYOUT-no-bare-modals`.
    11	
    12	## Choices
    13	
    14	- **Accent as punctuation, not fill:** the Undo action is accent-colored *text* on the elevated surface — the only accent on the page. The confirmation surface itself is monochrome (`bg-elev` + 1px `line` hairline border). No gradient, no glow, no drop shadow — elevation is carried by the hairline and the lighter surface, which fits the ink-on-paper brief.
    15	- **Placement:** fixed bottom-left with `--space-xl` margins — editorial rather than the centered-toast default (`OTHER-no-shadcn-defaults`).
    16	- **Behavior:** optimistic — the setting value flips immediately on Save; the snackbar confirms with inline **Undo** (reverts to the captured previous value), an explicit **dismiss** (X, `currentColor` stroke icon), and an 8s auto-dismiss. The timer pauses on hover and on keyboard focus within the bar and resumes with remaining time, so Undo can't vanish under the user's cursor. A new save replaces the current snackbar rather than stacking.
    17	- **A11y:** persistent `role="status" aria-live="polite"` region (the live region exists before content is injected, so announcements fire reliably); both actions are real `<button>`s with `min-height/min-width: 44px`; dismiss has an `aria-label`; visible `:focus-visible` outline; `prefers-reduced-motion` disables transitions in CSS *and* skips the JS exit animation.
    18	- **Dynamic DOM, not CSS hide:** the snackbar is created on save and removed from the DOM after the exit transition (with a timeout fallback in case `transitionend` never fires), per `OTHER-dynamic-dom-not-css-hide`.
    19	- **Tokens:** all DESIGN.md values defined as `:root` custom properties; every component declaration is `var(--token, fallback)` — no bare hex/px in component CSS. Buttons are semantic classes (`.btn`, `.btn-primary`, `.btn-undo`, `.btn-dismiss`) with hover, active, focus-visible, and disabled styling.
    20	- **Fonts:** `--font-body` names Untitled Sans with a Helvetica fallback stack (licensed woff2 files are not available in this fixture to inline); no italics, weights limited to 400/500/700; no serif anywhere (nothing authorial on this surface).
    21	- **Demo harness:** one hairline-bounded setting row ("Project visibility" Private/Public) plus a single "Save change" primary button, so save → undo produces a visibly verifiable state change. Copy is plain and concrete, no persuasion.
    22	
    23	## audit_taste result
    24	
    25	`audit_taste` (profile "andrew", surface "component-demo", html mode): **Verdict: PASS — no findings, none suppressed.** All deterministic detectors clean (no gradients, no glow shadows, no second accent hue, no banned words, no bare literals). 29 judgment-only rules reported honestly as `not_assessed` (no deterministic detector); each of the applicable ones was addressed by construction as listed above. `COLOR-one-warm-orange-accent` was skipped as out-of-scope for this surface (portfolio-monochrome scope) — the build honors it anyway with `#ed4609` as the sole accent.
    26	
    27	## Deviations
    28	
    29	- Untitled Sans/Geist are referenced by token with system fallbacks rather than embedded, since the font files are not present in the fixture directory and the artifact must make no external requests.

   501	Also: `audit` is a surface-detecting fan-out with a fixed input schema — `url`, `html`, `nodes`, `source`, `screenshot`, `diff`, `surface`, `intent`, `project`, `profile` (`src/index.ts:7721-7730`), all optional.
